Analyze the protein sequence:**<br>The protein is 213 amino acids long, with a hydrophilic N-terminus (MYPHRRADMNPISEFIRGITKDNPTFG) and a hydrophilic C-terminus (NYRRQQKAAKGG). Both termini are hydrophilic, suggesting an even number of membrane crossings (both ends on the same side).<br><br>**2. Identify hydrophobic segments (TM candidates):**<br><br>- **TM1 (~28-48):** LVLGLCPTLAVTTSVENGIG — moderately hydrophobic, sufficient for membrane crossing<br>- **TM2 (~47-66):** GIGMAMGTLFVLVGSNMMVSA — strongly hydrophobic<br>- **TM3 (~78-96):** EIIVIATFVTIVDMVMEA — strongly hydrophobic<br>- **TM4 (~105-122):** LGVFIPLIVVNCIVIGRAE — strongly hydrophobic<br>- **TM5 (~143-158):** GTGFLLVLILIGGIRELL — very hydrophobic<br>- **TM6 (~190-203):** GAFLTIAVLMTIVN — moderately hydrophobic<br><br>**3. Evaluate alternatives:**<br>- 4 segments (A) would leave too many unaccounted hydrophobic regions<br>- 5 segments (B) would require excluding one clearly hydrophobic stretch<br>- 7 segments (D) would require odd passes, contradicting the hydrophilic termini on the same side<br><br>**4. Biological context:**<br>This protein resembles members of the SulP/anion transporter or formylmethionine transporter families, which commonly have 6 transmembrane helices. The question also mentions &quot;family-specific sequence evidence&quot; which points to the IMPDH family motif I identified. The &quot;most specific biological process&quot; that IMPDH supports is de novo purine nucleotide biosynthesis.
